---------- turn 12 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it’s mostly work. I mean, I work as a buyer, so I’m always looking at orders and launch numbers and vendor stuff, and lately I just can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers on purpose, like to make me look stupid or incompetent.\n\nAnd I know how that sounds, because my supervisor has sat down with me and shown me reports and been like, “Look, this doesn’t prove anyone’s doing anything,” but it doesn’t really settle it for me. I’ll look at the numbers and it just feels like there’s something underneath it, like they’re hiding it well or changing things just enough so I can’t point to one clean piece of proof. It’s been getting in my head a lot, and I figured I should talk to someone before it gets worse or I do something at work that makes everything blow up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s the part I keep getting stuck on. I don’t know if I’d say it like some big dramatic “plot,” like in a movie or something, but it does feel like it’s aimed at me specifically. Like they’re watching what I’m buying, what I’m choosing, and then the numbers around the launches get weird in ways that make me look bad — like I don’t know what I’m doing or I’m making dumb calls.\n\nAnd the frustrating thing is, my supervisor has gone over reports with me and tried to show me, “See, this doesn’t prove anyone is doing that,” and I can sit there and understand what he’s saying on paper. But it doesn’t really make the feeling go away. It’s like… okay, maybe the report doesn’t show it clearly, but that could just mean they’re careful about it, or they’re changing things just enough that I can’t point to one obvious thing and say, “There, that’s it.”\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do feel like people at work — and even some vendors — are messing with things to make me look incompetent. That’s really what made me come in, because I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people at work plotting against you are definitely true, even if you can't prove them?\n\nPatient: I mean… “definitely” is hard, because I know I don’t have that one clean piece of proof where I can lay it on the table and say, there, that proves it. My supervisor has shown me reports and gone through the numbers with me, and I can see what he’s trying to show me — like, on paper, it doesn’t prove that anyone is doing anything.\n\nBut it still feels true to me. That’s the problem. I can’t shake it. It feels like people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking what I’m buying and then messing with the launch numbers just enough to make me look stupid or incompetent. Like they know exactly how to make it look like it’s just normal business stuff or normal errors, but when I’m looking at it, it feels targeted.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if I can say “definitely” in the sense of proving it, but in my head it feels like it’s happening. Even when someone shows me reports that don’t back it up, I keep thinking maybe that just means they’re hiding it well. That’s really what made me come in, because I can’t get myself to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs of yours could possibly be false, or do you believe they are absolutely true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I have to say it could possibly be false, because I don’t have proof-proof. Like, my supervisor has literally sat with me and pulled up the reports and gone through them, and on the surface they don’t show some obvious thing where I can say, “Okay, that vendor changed this number” or “this person at work did that.” I can understand that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t feel false. That’s the issue. It feels like people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and then messing with the launch numbers just enough to make me look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing. And when the reports don’t prove it, my mind doesn’t go, “Oh, okay, then it’s not happening.” It goes, “Well, maybe they’re covering it, or maybe it’s being done in a way that won’t show up cleanly.”\n\nSo… absolutely true? I don’t know if I should say it that strongly. But it feels true enough that I can’t let it go, even when people show me reasons I should.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about people at work plotting against you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months, I think. I mean, not like I woke up one day and suddenly had the whole thing fully formed in my head, but roughly ten months since I started really noticing it and not being able to brush it off.\n\nAt first it was more like, “That’s weird,” or “Why did that number change like that?” and I’d try to explain it away. But then it kept happening, or at least it kept feeling like there was a pattern, and over time it got harder to ignore. So if you’re asking how long I’ve been having the suspicious thoughts about work and people messing with things, I’d say around ten months.\n\nAnd the voice — that’s been around that same amount of time too, roughly. Not exactly to the day or anything, but it’s all been going on for most of the past year.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about people plotting against you at work affecting your ability to do your job or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. I wish I could say it was just in my head and I was still functioning normally, but my job has taken a real hit from it.\n\nI’ve missed buying deadlines because I get stuck checking things over and over or trying to figure out if someone changed something. And then sometimes I go the other direction and make these fast purchasing calls because I’m trying to get ahead of what I think people are doing, or stop them from making me look bad, and then afterward it turns out I probably should’ve waited or talked it through. My supervisor formally warned me about it, which was humiliating, honestly. He basically said the missed deadlines and some of the vendor issues can’t keep happening.\n\nAnd the vendor relationships… that’s been bad too. I’ve gotten tense with them, questioned them too much, or acted like I didn’t trust what they were telling me, and I know that’s damaged things. Even when I’m trying to be professional, I think it comes through that I’m suspicious.\n\nWith coworkers, I’ve pulled back a lot. I don’t chat the same way. I avoid certain people, I don’t really joke around, and I’m always watching what I say because I feel like it could be used against me somehow. So yeah, interacting with them is harder.\n\nAnd outside of work too, I’ve kind of disappeared from people. I used to go to gallery nights with friends and play volleyball on weekends, and I’ve stopped showing up for a lot of that. I tell myself I’m busy or tired, but really I just don’t feel like being around people much. So… yeah, it’s definitely affecting things.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that people at work are plotting against you as something that most people would find hard to believe or unusual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, if I step outside myself for a second, I can see that most people would probably say it sounds hard to believe. Like, if someone else told me, “People at work and vendors are tracking my buys and changing launch numbers just to make me look stupid,” I’d probably think, okay, that sounds like a lot. I get that.\n\nBut when I’m in it, it doesn’t feel unusual. It feels like I’m noticing something other people are missing or don’t want to take seriously. That’s what’s frustrating, because my supervisor can show me the reports and say, “Look, there’s nothing here proving that,” and I can understand what he’s saying, but it still doesn’t make the feeling go away. It’s like the fact that it doesn’t show up clearly almost makes me wonder if they’re being careful about it.\n\nSo… yes, I know most people would probably find it unusual or hard to believe. That’s part of why I’m here, honestly. I can hear how it sounds, but I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people plotting against you at work changed in intensity or certainty over the past ten months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. It wasn’t as strong at the beginning. At first I could kind of talk myself down, like, “Okay, maybe I’m reading too much into this,” or “Maybe the numbers were entered wrong,” or “Maybe the vendor just made a mistake.” I could still sort of put it in the category of normal work problems.\n\nBut over the months it’s gotten more… fixed, I guess. Like now when I see something off with my buys or the launch numbers, my mind goes straight to, “They’re doing it again,” like people at work or even certain vendors are tracking what I’m doing and messing with things so I look incompetent. And even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that, it doesn’t really settle it for me. I can look at the report and understand what he’s pointing to, but inside I’m still thinking, “That doesn’t mean it’s not happening.”\n\nSo the certainty has gone up, yeah. I don’t know if I’d say it’s one hundred percent every second of the day, because there are moments where I think, “This sounds crazy, maybe I’m wrong,” but those moments don’t last very long. The feeling comes back pretty hard. That’s really what made me come in — I can’t shake it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your feelings of being plotted against could be a result of your own misinterpretation or misunderstanding of the situations at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say there’s a possibility, yeah. I’m trying to be honest about that. Part of me knows I could be misunderstanding things or connecting dots that aren’t actually connected.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel that way when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some vendors — are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Like I’ll see something off, or something won’t match the way I expected, and my brain just goes straight there.\n\nAnd even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says, basically, “This doesn’t prove anyone is doing that,” I can see the report, I can understand what he’s saying… but it doesn’t really land. It doesn’t make the feeling go away. I’ll still think, well, maybe it just doesn’t show up there, or maybe they’re being careful.\n\nSo yes, I guess there’s some possibility I’m misreading things. That’s why I’m here. But I don’t feel convinced by that explanation, if that makes sense. The feeling keeps coming back like it’s real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you found out for certain that there was no evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: If I found out for certain… I mean, I want to say yes. I want to say if someone could really prove to me there was no evidence, then I’d be able to let it go. That would be the reasonable answer.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know if it would change right away. Because that’s kind of already been happening in a smaller way. My supervisor has shown me reports and basically said, “Look, this doesn’t prove anyone is tracking your buys or changing the launch numbers,” and I can look at it and understand what he’s saying. I’m not sitting there unable to read it or anything. I get the explanation.\n\nBut the feeling still stays there. It’s like my mind goes, okay, maybe that report doesn’t show it, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that wouldn’t be obvious, or maybe the vendors and people at work know how to keep it from looking direct. And I know how that sounds. I know it sounds like I’m moving the goalposts, but that’s what’s scary about it — even when someone gives me something that should calm me down, it doesn’t really calm me down.\n\nSo if it was truly certain, like somehow completely impossible that anyone was messing with things, maybe part of me would try to accept that. But I think another part of me would still be suspicious for a while. I can’t shake it that easily. That’s really why I came in, because it’s not just a passing thought anymore. It keeps coming back that people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the plot you think is happening against you as something that could realistically happen in everyday life?\n\nPatient: I guess… that’s part of what makes it hard for me, because it doesn’t feel impossible. Like, if I was saying something totally out there, maybe I could dismiss it easier. But people at work do track buys. Vendors do send numbers and reports, and launch numbers can get adjusted or entered wrong or explained in different ways. So in my head it’s like, yes, this could happen in real life.\n\nBut then it goes further for me. It’s not just, “Maybe there was a mistake.” It’s that I can’t shake the feeling that certain people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers on purpose so I look stupid or incompetent. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on.\n\nAnd my supervisor has shown me reports and tried to explain that they don’t prove anyone is doing that. I can sit there and understand what he’s showing me, but it doesn’t really make the thought go away. I’ll still think maybe the report just doesn’t show it, or maybe they’re being careful about how they do it.\n\nSo… realistically, could someone mess with numbers or track buys? Yeah, I think that could happen. But I also know the way I’m holding onto it is probably more intense than other people would. Still, it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if someone you completely trusted told you there was no plot?\n\nPatient: If it was someone I completely trusted… I mean, I think it would matter. I wouldn’t just brush it off the same way. Like if it was someone I really believed had no reason to lie to me, part of me would want to listen and calm down.\n\nBut I don’t know if it would make the belief just disappear. That’s the part that bothers me. Because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and explains that they don’t prove anyone is tracking my buys or changing the launch numbers, I can understand what he’s saying in the moment. I can follow the logic. But then later, or even while he’s talking, this other thought comes in like, okay, but what if the report just doesn’t show it? What if the vendors are involved too, or what if people are being careful so it looks normal?\n\nSo if someone I trusted said, “There’s no plot,” I think I’d probably try to believe them. I might even feel a little relieved for a bit. But I don’t think it would fully settle it for me right away, because the feeling is so stuck in my head — that people at work, and maybe vendors too, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. It keeps coming back even when I’m given reasons it shouldn’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["F22", "F23"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some vendors — are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Like I’ll see something off, or something won’t match the way I expected, and my brain just goes straight there.\n\nAnd even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says, basically, “This doesn’t prove anyone is doing that,” I can see the report, I can understand what he’s saying… but it doesn’t really land. It doesn’t make the feeling go away. I’ll still think, well, maybe it just doesn’t show up there, or maybe they’re being careful.\n\nSo yes, I guess there’s some possibility I’m misreading things. That’s why I’m here. But I don’t feel convinced by that explanation, if that makes sense. The feeling keeps coming back like it’s real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you found out for certain that there was no evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: If I found out for certain… I mean, I want to say yes. I want to say if someone could really prove to me there was no evidence, then I’d be able to let it go. That would be the reasonable answer.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know if it would change right away. Because that’s kind of already been happening in a smaller way. My supervisor has shown me reports and basically said, “Look, this doesn’t prove anyone is tracking your buys or changing the launch numbers,” and I can look at it and understand what he’s saying. I’m not sitting there unable to read it or anything. I get the explanation.\n\nBut the feeling still stays there. And I know how that sounds. I know it sounds like I’m moving the goalposts, but that’s what’s scary about it — even when someone gives me something that should calm me down, it doesn’t really calm me down.\n\nSo if it was truly certain, like somehow completely impossible that anyone was messing with things, maybe part of me would try to accept that. It keeps coming back that people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the plot you think is happening against you as something that could realistically happen in everyday life?\n\nPatient: I guess… that’s part of what makes it hard for me, because it doesn’t feel impossible. It’s not just, “Maybe there was a mistake.” It’s that I can’t shake the feeling that certain people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers on purpose so I look stupid or incompetent. Yeah, I think that could happen. But I also know the way I’m holding onto it is probably more intense than other people would. Still, it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if someone you completely trusted told you there was no plot?\n\nPatient: If it was someone I completely trusted… I mean, I think it would matter. I wouldn’t just brush it off the same way. I’ve thought about that, yeah. I mean… I know work has been stressful, and I know when I’m stressed I probably read into things more. So part of me can say, maybe this is my anxiety about work making everything feel more threatening than it is.

But then it doesn’t feel like just anxiety when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. Like they’re setting it up so it looks like I don’t know what I’m doing.

And I know my supervisor has tried to show me the reports and explain that they don’t actually prove that anyone is doing that. I can sit there and look at the reports and understand what he’s saying. But the thought doesn’t go away. It’s like my brain keeps finding another angle, like maybe the report wouldn’t show it, or maybe they changed it in a way that looks normal.

So… yes, I can admit it’s possible stress is making it worse. I don’t want to pretend I’m completely objective about it. But it still feels real to me, and that’s the problem. Even when I try to tell myself it could just be work stress, the suspicion comes right back.

That’s the issue. It feels like people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and then messing with the launch numbers just enough to make me look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing. And when the reports don’t prove it, my mind doesn’t go, “Oh, okay, then it’s not happening.” It goes, “Well, maybe they’re covering it, or maybe it’s being done in a way that won’t show up cleanly.”\n\nSo… absolutely true? I don’t know if I should say it that strongly. But it feels true enough that I can’t let it go, even when people show me reasons I should.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about people at work plotting against you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months, I think. I mean, not like I woke up one day and suddenly had the whole thing fully formed in my head, but roughly ten months since I started really noticing it and not being able to brush it off.\n\nAt first it was more like, “That’s weird,” or “Why did that number change like that?” and I’d try to explain it away. But then it kept happening, or at least it kept feeling like there was a pattern, and over time it got harder to ignore. So if you’re asking how long I’ve been having the suspicious thoughts about work and people messing with things, I’d say around ten months.\n\nAnd the voice — that’s been around that same amount of time too, roughly. Not exactly to the day or anything, but it’s all been going on for most of the past year.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about people plotting against you at work affecting your ability to do your job or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. I wish I could say it was just in my head and I was still functioning normally, but my job has taken a real hit from it.\n\nI’ve missed buying deadlines because I get stuck checking things over and over or trying to figure out if someone changed something. And then sometimes I go the other direction and make these fast purchasing calls because I’m trying to get ahead of what I think people are doing, or stop them from making me look bad, and then afterward it turns out I probably should’ve waited or talked it through. My supervisor formally warned me about it, which was humiliating, honestly. He basically said the missed deadlines and some of the vendor issues can’t keep happening.\n\nAnd the vendor relationships… that’s been bad too. I’ve gotten tense with them, questioned them too much, or acted like I didn’t trust what they were telling me, and I know that’s damaged things. Even when I’m trying to be professional, I think it comes through that I’m suspicious.\n\nWith coworkers, I’ve pulled back a lot. I don’t chat the same way. I avoid certain people, I don’t really joke around, and I’m always watching what I say because I feel like it could be used against me somehow. So yeah, interacting with them is harder.\n\nAnd outside of work too, I’ve kind of disappeared from people. I used to go to gallery nights with friends and play volleyball on weekends, and I’ve stopped showing up for a lot of that. I tell myself I’m busy or tired, but really I just don’t feel like being around people much. So… yeah, it’s definitely affecting things.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that people at work are plotting against you as something that most people would find hard to believe or unusual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, if I step outside myself for a second, I can see that most people would probably say it sounds hard to believe. Like, if someone else told me, “People at work and vendors are tracking my buys and changing launch numbers just to make me look stupid,” I’d probably think, okay, that sounds like a lot. I get that.\n\nBut when I’m in it, it doesn’t feel unusual. It feels like I’m noticing something other people are missing or don’t want to take seriously. That’s what’s frustrating, because my supervisor can show me the reports and say, “Look, there’s nothing here proving that,” and I can understand what he’s saying, but it still doesn’t make the feeling go away. It’s like the fact that it doesn’t show up clearly almost makes me wonder if they’re being careful about it.\n\nSo… yes, I know most people would probably find it unusual or hard to believe. That’s part of why I’m here, honestly. I can hear how it sounds, but I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people plotting against you at work changed in intensity or certainty over the past ten months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. It wasn’t as strong at the beginning. At first I could kind of talk myself down, like, “Okay, maybe I’m reading too much into this,” or “Maybe the numbers were entered wrong,” or “Maybe the vendor just made a mistake.” I could still sort of put it in the category of normal work problems.\n\nBut over the months it’s gotten more… fixed, I guess. Like now when I see something off with my buys or the launch numbers, my mind goes straight to, “They’re doing it again,” like people at work or even certain vendors are tracking what I’m doing and messing with things so I look incompetent. And even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that, it doesn’t really settle it for me. I can look at the report and understand what he’s pointing to, but inside I’m still thinking, “That doesn’t mean it’s not happening.”\n\nSo the certainty has gone up, yeah. I don’t know if I’d say it’s one hundred percent every second of the day, because there are moments where I think, “This sounds crazy, maybe I’m wrong,” but those moments don’t last very long. The feeling comes back pretty hard. That’s really what made me come in — I can’t shake it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your feelings of being plotted against could be a result of your own misinterpretation or misunderstanding of the situations at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say there’s a possibility, yeah. I’m trying to be honest about that. Part of me knows I could be misunderstanding things or connecting dots that aren’t actually connected.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel that way when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some vendors — are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Like I’ll see something off, or something won’t match the way I expected, and my brain just goes straight there.\n\nAnd even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says, basically, “This doesn’t prove anyone is doing that,” I can see the report, I can understand what he’s saying… but it doesn’t really land. It doesn’t make the feeling go away. I’ll still think, well, maybe it just doesn’t show up there, or maybe they’re being careful.\n\nSo yes, I guess there’s some possibility I’m misreading things. That’s why I’m here. But I don’t feel convinced by that explanation, if that makes sense. The feeling keeps coming back like it’s real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you found out for certain that there was no evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: If I found out for certain… I mean, I want to say yes. I want to say if someone could really prove to me there was no evidence, then I’d be able to let it go. That would be the reasonable answer.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know if it would change right away. Because that’s kind of already been happening in a smaller way. My supervisor has shown me reports and basically said, “Look, this doesn’t prove anyone is tracking your buys or changing the launch numbers,” and I can look at it and understand what he’s saying. I’m not sitting there unable to read it or anything. I get the explanation.\n\nBut the feeling still stays there. It’s like my mind goes, okay, maybe that report doesn’t show it, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that wouldn’t be obvious, or maybe the vendors and people at work know how to keep it from looking direct. And I know how that sounds. I know it sounds like I’m moving the goalposts, but that’s what’s scary about it — even when someone gives me something that should calm me down, it doesn’t really calm me down.\n\nSo if it was truly certain, like somehow completely impossible that anyone was messing with things, maybe part of me would try to accept that. But I think another part of me would still be suspicious for a while. I can’t shake it that easily. That’s really why I came in, because it’s not just a passing thought anymore. It keeps coming back that people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the plot you think is happening against you as something that could realistically happen in everyday life?\n\nPatient: I guess… that’s part of what makes it hard for me, because it doesn’t feel impossible. Like, if I was saying something totally out there, maybe I could dismiss it easier. But people at work do track buys. Vendors do send numbers and reports, and launch numbers can get adjusted or entered wrong or explained in different ways. So in my head it’s like, yes, this could happen in real life.\n\nBut then it goes further for me. It’s not just, “Maybe there was a mistake.” It’s that I can’t shake the feeling that certain people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers on purpose so I look stupid or incompetent. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on.\n\nAnd my supervisor has shown me reports and tried to explain that they don’t prove anyone is doing that. I can sit there and understand what he’s showing me, but it doesn’t really make the thought go away. I’ll still think maybe the report just doesn’t show it, or maybe they’re being careful about how they do it.\n\nSo… realistically, could someone mess with numbers or track buys? Yeah, I think that could happen. But I also know the way I’m holding onto it is probably more intense than other people would. Still, it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if someone you completely trusted told you there was no plot?\n\nPatient: If it was someone I completely trusted… I mean, I think it would matter. I wouldn’t just brush it off the same way. Like if it was someone I really believed had no reason to lie to me, part of me would want to listen and calm down.\n\nBut I don’t know if it would make the belief just disappear. That’s the part that bothers me. Because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and explains that they don’t prove anyone is tracking my buys or changing the launch numbers, I can understand what he’s saying in the moment. I can follow the logic. But then later, or even while he’s talking, this other thought comes in like, okay, but what if the report just doesn’t show it? What if the vendors are involved too, or what if people are being careful so it looks normal?\n\nSo if someone I trusted said, “There’s no plot,” I think I’d probably try to believe them. I might even feel a little relieved for a bit. But I don’t think it would fully settle it for me right away, because the feeling is so stuck in my head — that people at work, and maybe vendors too, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. It keeps coming back even when I’m given reasons it shouldn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your feelings of being plotted against could be related to your own stress or anxiety about work rather than an actual plot?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah. I mean… I know work has been stressful, and I know when I’m stressed I probably read into things more. So part of me can say, maybe this is my anxiety about work making everything feel more threatening than it is.\n\nBut then it doesn’t feel like just anxiety when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. Like they’re setting it up so it looks like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know my supervisor has tried to show me the reports and explain that they don’t actually prove that anyone is doing that. I can sit there and look at the reports and understand what he’s saying. But the thought doesn’t go away. It’s like my brain keeps finding another angle, like maybe the report wouldn’t show it, or maybe they changed it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo… yes, I can admit it’s possible stress is making it worse. I don’t want to pretend I’m completely objective about it. But it still feels real to me, and that’s the problem. Even when I try to tell myself it could just be work stress, the suspicion comes right back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Maybe for parts of a day, but not really a whole day where it’s just gone. It’s more like it quiets down if I’m busy or distracted, or if I’m not looking at the numbers right then. Like I can be doing something else and not actively thinking, “They’re plotting against me,” every single second.

And then I’m back to thinking people at work, and maybe some of the vendors too, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.

That’s why it’s been hard, because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that is happening, I can understand what he means, but it doesn’t stay settled in my head. So I guess… no, I don’t think I’ve had a full day recently where I truly didn’t believe it at all. More like moments where I’m not focused on it, and then it comes back.

But it doesn’t really make the feeling go away. It’s like… okay, maybe the report doesn’t show it clearly, but that could just mean they’re careful about it, or they’re changing things just enough that I can’t point to one obvious thing and say, “There, that’s it.”\n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do feel like people at work — and even some vendors — are messing with things to make me look incompetent. That’s really what made me come in, because I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people at work plotting against you are definitely true, even if you can't prove them?\n\nPatient: I mean… “definitely” is hard, because I know I don’t have that one clean piece of proof where I can lay it on the table and say, there, that proves it. My supervisor has shown me reports and gone through the numbers with me, and I can see what he’s trying to show me — like, on paper, it doesn’t prove that anyone is doing anything.\n\nBut it still feels true to me. That’s the problem. I can’t shake it. It feels like people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking what I’m buying and then messing with the launch numbers just enough to make me look stupid or incompetent. Like they know exactly how to make it look like it’s just normal business stuff or normal errors, but when I’m looking at it, it feels targeted.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if I can say “definitely” in the sense of proving it, but in my head it feels like it’s happening. Even when someone shows me reports that don’t back it up, I keep thinking maybe that just means they’re hiding it well. That’s really what made me come in, because I can’t get myself to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs of yours could possibly be false, or do you believe they are absolutely true?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I have to say it could possibly be false, because I don’t have proof-proof. Like, my supervisor has literally sat with me and pulled up the reports and gone through them, and on the surface they don’t show some obvious thing where I can say, “Okay, that vendor changed this number” or “this person at work did that.” I can understand that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t feel false. That’s the issue. It feels like people at work, and even some vendors, are tracking my buys and then messing with the launch numbers just enough to make me look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing. And when the reports don’t prove it, my mind doesn’t go, “Oh, okay, then it’s not happening.” It goes, “Well, maybe they’re covering it, or maybe it’s being done in a way that won’t show up cleanly.”\n\nSo… absolutely true? I don’t know if I should say it that strongly. But then it kept happening, or at least it kept feeling like there was a pattern, and over time it got harder to ignore. So if you’re asking how long I’ve been having the suspicious thoughts about work and people messing with things, I’d say around ten months.\n\nAnd the voice — that’s been around that same amount of time too, roughly. Not exactly to the day or anything, but it’s all been going on for most of the past year.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about people plotting against you at work affecting your ability to do your job or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. I wish I could say it was just in my head and I was still functioning normally, but my job has taken a real hit from it.\n\nI’ve missed buying deadlines because I get stuck checking things over and over or trying to figure out if someone changed something. And then sometimes I go the other direction and make these fast purchasing calls because I’m trying to get ahead of what I think people are doing, or stop them from making me look bad, and then afterward it turns out I probably should’ve waited or talked it through. My supervisor formally warned me about it, which was humiliating, honestly. He basically said the missed deadlines and some of the vendor issues can’t keep happening.\n\nAnd the vendor relationships… that’s been bad too. I’ve gotten tense with them, questioned them too much, or acted like I didn’t trust what they were telling me, and I know that’s damaged things. Even when I’m trying to be professional, I think it comes through that I’m suspicious.\n\nWith coworkers, I’ve pulled back a lot. I don’t chat the same way. I avoid certain people, I don’t really joke around, and I’m always watching what I say because I feel like it could be used against me somehow. So yeah, interacting with them is harder.\n\nAnd outside of work too, I’ve kind of disappeared from people. I used to go to gallery nights with friends and play volleyball on weekends, and I’ve stopped showing up for a lot of that. I tell myself I’m busy or tired, but really I just don’t feel like being around people much. So… yeah, it’s definitely affecting things.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that people at work are plotting against you as something that most people would find hard to believe or unusual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, if I step outside myself for a second, I can see that most people would probably say it sounds hard to believe. Like, if someone else told me, “People at work and vendors are tracking my buys and changing launch numbers just to make me look stupid,” I’d probably think, okay, that sounds like a lot. I get that.\n\nBut when I’m in it, it doesn’t feel unusual. It feels like I’m noticing something other people are missing or don’t want to take seriously. That’s what’s frustrating, because my supervisor can show me the reports and say, “Look, there’s nothing here proving that,” and I can understand what he’s saying, but it still doesn’t make the feeling go away. It’s like the fact that it doesn’t show up clearly almost makes me wonder if they’re being careful about it.\n\nSo… yes, I know most people would probably find it unusual or hard to believe. That’s part of why I’m here, honestly. I can hear how it sounds, but I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people plotting against you at work changed in intensity or certainty over the past ten months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. It wasn’t as strong at the beginning. At first I could kind of talk myself down, like, “Okay, maybe I’m reading too much into this,” or “Maybe the numbers were entered wrong,” or “Maybe the vendor just made a mistake.” I could still sort of put it in the category of normal work problems.\n\nBut over the months it’s gotten more… fixed, I guess. Like now when I see something off with my buys or the launch numbers, my mind goes straight to, “They’re doing it again,” like people at work or even certain vendors are tracking what I’m doing and messing with things so I look incompetent. And even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that, it doesn’t really settle it for me. I can look at the report and understand what he’s pointing to, but inside I’m still thinking, “That doesn’t mean it’s not happening.”\n\nSo the certainty has gone up, yeah. The feeling comes back pretty hard. That’s really what made me come in — I can’t shake it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your feelings of being plotted against could be a result of your own misinterpretation or misunderstanding of the situations at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say there’s a possibility, yeah. I’m trying to be honest about that. Part of me knows I could be misunderstanding things or connecting dots that aren’t actually connected.\n\nBut the problem is, it doesn’t feel that way when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some vendors — are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid. Like I’ll see something off, or something won’t match the way I expected, and my brain just goes straight there.\n\nAnd even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says, basically, “This doesn’t prove anyone is doing that,” I can see the report, I can understand what he’s saying… but it doesn’t really land. It doesn’t make the feeling go away. I’ll still think, well, maybe it just doesn’t show up there, or maybe they’re being careful.\n\nSo yes, I guess there’s some possibility I’m misreading things. That’s why I’m here. But I don’t feel convinced by that explanation, if that makes sense. The feeling keeps coming back like it’s real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you found out for certain that there was no evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: If I found out for certain… I mean, I want to say yes. I want to say if someone could really prove to me there was no evidence, then I’d be able to let it go. That would be the reasonable answer.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know if it would change right away. Because that’s kind of already been happening in a smaller way. My supervisor has shown me reports and basically said, “Look, this doesn’t prove anyone is tracking your buys or changing the launch numbers,” and I can look at it and understand what he’s saying. I’m not sitting there unable to read it or anything. I get the explanation.\n\nBut the feeling still stays there. It’s like my mind goes, okay, maybe that report doesn’t show it, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that wouldn’t be obvious, or maybe the vendors and people at work know how to keep it from looking direct. And I know how that sounds. I know it sounds like I’m moving the goalposts, but that’s what’s scary about it — even when someone gives me something that should calm me down, it doesn’t really calm me down.\n\nSo if it was truly certain, like somehow completely impossible that anyone was messing with things, maybe part of me would try to accept that. But I think another part of me would still be suspicious for a while. I can’t shake it that easily. That’s really why I came in, because it’s not just a passing thought anymore. It keeps coming back that people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the plot you think is happening against you as something that could realistically happen in everyday life?\n\nPatient: I guess… that’s part of what makes it hard for me, because it doesn’t feel impossible. Like, if I was saying something totally out there, maybe I could dismiss it easier. But people at work do track buys. Vendors do send numbers and reports, and launch numbers can get adjusted or entered wrong or explained in different ways. So in my head it’s like, yes, this could happen in real life.\n\nBut then it goes further for me. It’s not just, “Maybe there was a mistake.” It’s that I can’t shake the feeling that certain people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers on purpose so I look stupid or incompetent. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on.\n\nAnd my supervisor has shown me reports and tried to explain that they don’t prove anyone is doing that. I can sit there and understand what he’s showing me, but it doesn’t really make the thought go away. I’ll still think maybe the report just doesn’t show it, or maybe they’re being careful about how they do it.\n\nSo… realistically, could someone mess with numbers or track buys? Yeah, I think that could happen. But I also know the way I’m holding onto it is probably more intense than other people would. Still, it feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if someone you completely trusted told you there was no plot?\n\nPatient: If it was someone I completely trusted… I mean, I think it would matter. I wouldn’t just brush it off the same way. Like if it was someone I really believed had no reason to lie to me, part of me would want to listen and calm down.\n\nBut I don’t know if it would make the belief just disappear. That’s the part that bothers me. Because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and explains that they don’t prove anyone is tracking my buys or changing the launch numbers, I can understand what he’s saying in the moment. I can follow the logic. But then later, or even while he’s talking, this other thought comes in like, okay, but what if the report just doesn’t show it? What if the vendors are involved too, or what if people are being careful so it looks normal?\n\nSo if someone I trusted said, “There’s no plot,” I think I’d probably try to believe them. I might even feel a little relieved for a bit. But I don’t think it would fully settle it for me right away, because the feeling is so stuck in my head — that people at work, and maybe vendors too, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. It keeps coming back even when I’m given reasons it shouldn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your feelings of being plotted against could be related to your own stress or anxiety about work rather than an actual plot?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah. I mean… I know work has been stressful, and I know when I’m stressed I probably read into things more. So part of me can say, maybe this is my anxiety about work making everything feel more threatening than it is.\n\nBut then it doesn’t feel like just anxiety when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. Like they’re setting it up so it looks like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know my supervisor has tried to show me the reports and explain that they don’t actually prove that anyone is doing that. I can sit there and look at the reports and understand what he’s saying. But the thought doesn’t go away. It’s like my brain keeps finding another angle, like maybe the report wouldn’t show it, or maybe they changed it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo… yes, I can admit it’s possible stress is making it worse. I don’t want to pretend I’m completely objective about it. But it still feels real to me, and that’s the problem. Even when I try to tell myself it could just be work stress, the suspicion comes right back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think people at work were plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Maybe for parts of a day, but not really a whole day where it’s just gone. It’s more like it quiets down if I’m busy or distracted, or if I’m not looking at the numbers right then. Like I can be doing something else and not actively thinking, “They’re plotting against me,” every single second.\n\nBut once something reminds me of it — a report, a vendor email, a launch number that doesn’t look right, or even someone at work talking in a way that seems a little off — it comes right back. And then I’m back to thinking people at work, and maybe some of the vendors too, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nThat’s why it’s been hard, because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that is happening, I can understand what he means, but it doesn’t stay settled in my head. So I guess… no, I don’t think I’ve had a full day recently where I truly didn’t believe it at all. More like moments where I’m not focused on it, and then it comes back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
If it was really indisputable… I mean, I want to say yes, because logically that should change it. If someone could show me something that completely proved no one at work was doing anything, no one was tracking my buys, no vendors were involved, nobody was messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid… then I would like to think I could let it go.

But I’m being honest, part of me worries I’d still start looking for a loophole. Like, my supervisor has already shown me reports and explained that they don’t prove what I think they prove, and in the moment I can sit there and understand what he’s saying. I’m not just ignoring him. I can follow the numbers, I can hear his explanation. But then the feeling comes back anyway, like maybe the reports don’t show the whole thing, or maybe there’s something being hidden, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that looks normal.

So if it was truly indisputable evidence, maybe it would help more than what I’ve been shown so far. I might calm down, at least for a while. But the thing that scares me is that this isn’t just a normal work concern anymore. It feels stuck, like I can’t shake it, even when I’m given reasons that should make me stop thinking it.

I might even feel a little relieved for a bit. It keeps coming back even when I’m given reasons it shouldn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your feelings of being plotted against could be related to your own stress or anxiety about work rather than an actual plot?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah. I mean… I know work has been stressful, and I know when I’m stressed I probably read into things more. So part of me can say, maybe this is my anxiety about work making everything feel more threatening than it is.\n\nBut then it doesn’t feel like just anxiety when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. Like they’re setting it up so it looks like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know my supervisor has tried to show me the reports and explain that they don’t actually prove that anyone is doing that. I can sit there and look at the reports and understand what he’s saying. But the thought doesn’t go away. I don’t want to pretend I’m completely objective about it. But it still feels real to me, and that’s the problem. Even when I try to tell myself it could just be work stress, the suspicion comes right back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think people at work were plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Maybe for parts of a day, but not really a whole day where it’s just gone. It’s more like it quiets down if I’m busy or distracted, or if I’m not looking at the numbers right then. Like I can be doing something else and not actively thinking, “They’re plotting against me,” every single second.\n\nBut once something reminds me of it — a report, a vendor email, a launch number that doesn’t look right, or even someone at work talking in a way that seems a little off — it comes right back. And then I’m back to thinking people at work, and maybe some of the vendors too, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nThat’s why it’s been hard, because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that is happening, I can understand what he means, but it doesn’t stay settled in my head. So I guess… no, I don’t think I’ve had a full day recently where I truly didn’t believe it at all. More like moments where I’m not focused on it, and then it comes back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you were presented with indisputable evidence that proved no one was plotting against you?\n\nPatient: If it was really indisputable… I mean, I want to say yes, because logically that should change it. If someone could show me something that completely proved no one at work was doing anything, no one was tracking my buys, no vendors were involved, nobody was messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid… then I would like to think I could let it go.\n\nBut I’m being honest, part of me worries I’d still start looking for a loophole. Like, my supervisor has already shown me reports and explained that they don’t prove what I think they prove, and in the moment I can sit there and understand what he’s saying. I’m not just ignoring him. I can follow the numbers, I can hear his explanation. But then the feeling comes back anyway, like maybe the reports don’t show the whole thing, or maybe there’s something being hidden, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo if it was truly indisputable evidence, maybe it would help more than what I’ve been shown so far. I might calm down, at least for a while. But the thing that scares me is that this isn’t just a normal work concern anymore. It feels stuck, like I can’t shake it, even when I’m given reasons that should make me stop thinking it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I can see how other people would call it irrational or unrealistic. I’m not just ignoring him. I can follow the numbers, I can hear his explanation. Even when I try to tell myself it could just be work stress, the suspicion comes right back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think people at work were plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Maybe for parts of a day, but not really a whole day where it’s just gone. It’s more like it quiets down if I’m busy or distracted, or if I’m not looking at the numbers right then. Like I can be doing something else and not actively thinking, “They’re plotting against me,” every single second.\n\nBut once something reminds me of it — a report, a vendor email, a launch number that doesn’t look right, or even someone at work talking in a way that seems a little off — it comes right back. And then I’m back to thinking people at work, and maybe some of the vendors too, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nThat’s why it’s been hard, because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that is happening, I can understand what he means, but it doesn’t stay settled in my head. So I guess… no, I don’t think I’ve had a full day recently where I truly didn’t believe it at all. More like moments where I’m not focused on it, and then it comes back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you were presented with indisputable evidence that proved no one was plotting against you?\n\nPatient: If it was really indisputable… I mean, I want to say yes, because logically that should change it. If someone could show me something that completely proved no one at work was doing anything, no one was tracking my buys, no vendors were involved, nobody was messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid… then I would like to think I could let it go.\n\nBut I’m being honest, part of me worries I’d still start looking for a loophole. Like, my supervisor has already shown me reports and explained that they don’t prove what I think they prove, and in the moment I can sit there and understand what he’s saying. I’m not just ignoring him. I can follow the numbers, I can hear his explanation. But then the feeling comes back anyway, like maybe the reports don’t show the whole thing, or maybe there’s something being hidden, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo if it was truly indisputable evidence, maybe it would help more than what I’ve been shown so far. I might calm down, at least for a while. But the thing that scares me is that this isn’t just a normal work concern anymore. It feels stuck, like I can’t shake it, even when I’m given reasons that should make me stop thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you could be considered irrational or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I can see how other people would call it irrational or unrealistic. I mean, if I step outside of myself for a second, I know how it sounds — like I’m saying people at work and even vendors are tracking what I buy and messing with launch numbers just to make me look stupid. That’s the hard part. I keep feeling like people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. I’m not saying I fully agree with that, because part of me still feels like something is going on, but I can understand why you’d use that word.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t know if I’d say it’s like… a huge organized thing with lots of people sitting in a room planning it out. That sounds extreme when I say it that way. But then it kept happening, or at least it kept feeling like there was a pattern, and over time it got harder to ignore. I used to go to gallery nights with friends and play volleyball on weekends, and I’ve stopped showing up for a lot of that. It’s like my mind goes, okay, maybe that report doesn’t show it, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that wouldn’t be obvious, or maybe the vendors and people at work know how to keep it from looking direct. So in my head it’s like, yes, this could happen in real life.\n\nBut then it goes further for me. It’s not just, “Maybe there was a mistake.” It’s that I can’t shake the feeling that certain people at work, and even some vendors, are watching what I’m buying and messing with the launch numbers on purpose so I look stupid or incompetent. That’s the part I keep getting stuck on.\n\nAnd my supervisor has shown me reports and tried to explain that they don’t prove anyone is doing that. I wouldn’t just brush it off the same way. Like if it was someone I really believed had no reason to lie to me, part of me would want to listen and calm down.\n\nBut I don’t know if it would make the belief just disappear. What if the vendors are involved too, or what if people are being careful so it looks normal?\n\nSo if someone I trusted said, “There’s no plot,” I think I’d probably try to believe them. I might even feel a little relieved for a bit. But I don’t think it would fully settle it for me right away, because the feeling is so stuck in my head — that people at work, and maybe vendors too, are watching what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. It keeps coming back even when I’m given reasons it shouldn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your feelings of being plotted against could be related to your own stress or anxiety about work rather than an actual plot?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah. I mean… I know work has been stressful, and I know when I’m stressed I probably read into things more. So part of me can say, maybe this is my anxiety about work making everything feel more threatening than it is.\n\nBut then it doesn’t feel like just anxiety when it’s happening. What really made me come in is that I can’t shake this feeling that people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. Like they’re setting it up so it looks like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know my supervisor has tried to show me the reports and explain that they don’t actually prove that anyone is doing that. I can sit there and look at the reports and understand what he’s saying. But the thought doesn’t go away. It’s like my brain keeps finding another angle, like maybe the report wouldn’t show it, or maybe they changed it in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo… yes, I can admit it’s possible stress is making it worse. I don’t want to pretend I’m completely objective about it. But it still feels real to me, and that’s the problem. Even when I try to tell myself it could just be work stress, the suspicion comes right back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think people at work were plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Maybe for parts of a day, but not really a whole day where it’s just gone. It’s more like it quiets down if I’m busy or distracted, or if I’m not looking at the numbers right then. Like I can be doing something else and not actively thinking, “They’re plotting against me,” every single second.\n\nBut once something reminds me of it — a report, a vendor email, a launch number that doesn’t look right, or even someone at work talking in a way that seems a little off — it comes right back. And then I’m back to thinking people at work, and maybe some of the vendors too, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid.\n\nThat’s why it’s been hard, because even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove anything like that is happening, I can understand what he means, but it doesn’t stay settled in my head. So I guess… no, I don’t think I’ve had a full day recently where I truly didn’t believe it at all. More like moments where I’m not focused on it, and then it comes back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would change if you were presented with indisputable evidence that proved no one was plotting against you?\n\nPatient: If it was really indisputable… I mean, I want to say yes, because logically that should change it. If someone could show me something that completely proved no one at work was doing anything, no one was tracking my buys, no vendors were involved, nobody was messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid… then I would like to think I could let it go.\n\nBut I’m being honest, part of me worries I’d still start looking for a loophole. Like, my supervisor has already shown me reports and explained that they don’t prove what I think they prove, and in the moment I can sit there and understand what he’s saying. I’m not just ignoring him. I can follow the numbers, I can hear his explanation. But then the feeling comes back anyway, like maybe the reports don’t show the whole thing, or maybe there’s something being hidden, or maybe the numbers were changed in a way that looks normal.\n\nSo if it was truly indisputable evidence, maybe it would help more than what I’ve been shown so far. I might calm down, at least for a while. But the thing that scares me is that this isn’t just a normal work concern anymore. It feels stuck, like I can’t shake it, even when I’m given reasons that should make me stop thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you could be considered irrational or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I can see how other people would call it irrational or unrealistic. I mean, if I step outside of myself for a second, I know how it sounds — like I’m saying people at work and even vendors are tracking what I buy and messing with launch numbers just to make me look stupid. I get that most people would probably say, “That’s a big leap,” or “Why would they go through all that?”\n\nAnd my supervisor has tried to show me the reports, and he says they don’t prove anything like that is happening. I can sit there and understand what he’s pointing at, like the numbers don’t directly show some plot. So yeah, I know other people might think I’m being unrealistic.\n\nBut at the same time, when I’m in it, it doesn’t feel irrational. It feels like I’m noticing things other people are dismissing. That’s the hard part. I can admit it might look unreasonable from the outside, but inside my head it still feels real, and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about people plotting against you would be considered delusions by a mental health professional?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, I think a mental health professional might call it that. I don’t really like saying that word, because it makes it sound like I’m just making it up or I’m completely out of touch, and that’s not how it feels to me. It feels like I’m seeing a pattern that matters.\n\nBut what really made me come in is exactly that I can’t shake it. I keep feeling like people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers so I look stupid. And even when my supervisor sits down with me and shows me the reports and says, “This doesn’t prove that,” I can understand what he’s showing me, but it doesn’t make the feeling go away.\n\nSo… if a professional heard that I still believe it even after being shown reasons not to, then yeah, I can see how they might consider it a delusion. I’m not saying I fully agree with that, because part of me still feels like something is going on, but I can understand why you’d use that word.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot you believe is happening against you is part of a larger, organized plan?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s like… a huge organized thing with lots of people sitting in a room planning it out. It feels like people at work and even some of the vendors are involved somehow, like they’re tracking what I buy and then the launch numbers come out in a way that makes me look stupid or incompetent.\n\nThat’s part of why I can’t let it go. If it were just one weird comment or one mistake in a report, maybe I could brush it off. But it feels connected, like the buys, the vendors, the numbers, the way things get presented afterward… like there’s a pattern. And then my supervisor shows me the reports and says they don’t prove that, and I can follow what he’s saying, but it still doesn’t make the feeling go away.\n\nSo… I guess yes, in the sense that it feels coordinated to me. I’m not sure how organized or how far it goes, but it doesn’t feel random.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say “highly organized” exactly, because that makes it sound like there’s some formal system or like I’ve got proof of a whole chain of people doing it step by step. I don’t have that kind of proof, and I know that’s part of why this sounds… I don’t know, intense.

But it does feel systematic to me in the sense that it keeps lining up around the same thing — my buys, the vendors, the launch numbers, and then somehow it makes me look stupid or like I don’t know what I’m doing. It’s not just one random mistake that happened once and I got embarrassed. It’s more like I keep seeing the same pattern, and that’s what I can’t get out of my head.

What really pushed me to come in is that I can’t shake it, even when my supervisor shows me the reports and says, basically, “Look, this doesn’t prove anyone is doing that.” I can sit there and understand what he’s saying, but it still feels like something is happening behind it. So… maybe not highly organized like a big operation, but coordinated enough that it feels deliberate to me.
